{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_01Jj5WjindqrxqYk7o1oQSQw","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01U1qkiwfNtxjbzHzi26R67u","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/workspace/documentation.md","content":"# Kubelet Container Manager Architecture Guide\n\n## 1. Subsystem Purpose and Boundaries\n\nThe kubelet container manager subsystem is responsible for managing the complete lifecycle of containers and pods on a Kubernetes node. It serves as the critical interface between the Kubernetes control plane's desired pod specifications and the actual container runtime (Docker, containerd, CRI-O, etc.) through the Container Runtime Interface (CRI).\n\n### Purpose\n\n- **Container Lifecycle Management**: Create, start, stop, and delete containers with proper ordering and resource allocation\n- **Resource Enforcement**: Apply CPU limits, memory reservations, and device allocations through cgroups\n- **Pod Orchestration**: Coordinate sandbox creation and container dependencies (init containers, ephemeral containers, app containers)\n- **State Reconciliation**: Drive containers toward desired state through repeated synchronization cycles\n- **Garbage Collection**: Remove unused containers and images to prevent resource exhaustion\n\n### Boundaries\n\n**Upstream Dependencies**:\n- **Pod Specifications** (`v1.Pod`): Desired pod configuration from API server\n- **Pod Status**: Current runtime state queried from container runtime\n- **Configuration Sources**: File-based manifests, API server, HTTP endpoints\n- **Resource Allocations**: From device plugins, CPU/memory managers, and topology managers\n\n**Downstream Dependencies**:\n- **Container Runtime Interface (CRI)**: gRPC service for pod/container operations\n- **Cgroup Managers** (Linux): Apply resource limits via cgroup filesystems\n- **Volume Mounts**: Storage subsystem for ephemeral and persistent volumes\n- **Network Plugins**: CNI for pod networking (handled separately in kubelet)\n\n**External Services**:\n- Image registry access for container image pulls\n- Kubelet's own node status and eviction managers\n- Pod lifecycle hooks (pre-start, post-stop handlers)\n\n## 2. Key Components and Responsibility Split\n\n### 2.1 Runtime Interface Layer\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/container/runtime.go`\n\nThe `Runtime` interface defines the contract that all container runtimes must implement:\n\n```\nRuntime interface provides:\n- SyncPod(ctx, pod, podStatus, pullSecrets, backOff, restartAllContainers) → PodSyncResult\n  Orchestrates pod creation/updates, the primary entry point\n\n- KillPod(ctx, pod, runningPod, gracePeriodOverride) → error\n  Terminates all containers in a pod with grace period\n\n- GetPodStatus(ctx, uid, name, namespace) → *PodStatus\n  Queries runtime for current pod state\n\n- GarbageCollect(ctx, gcPolicy, allSourcesReady, evictNonDeletedPods) → error\n  Cleans up dead containers per policy\n\n- ImageService: PullImage, ListImages, RemoveImage operations\n```\n\nThis interface allows multiple runtime implementations to be plugged in, though the dominant implementation is `kubeGenericRuntimeManager`.\n\n### 2.2 Concrete Runtime Manager\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/kuberuntime/kuberuntime_manager.go`\n\n`kubeGenericRuntimeManager` is the main orchestration component. It holds:\n\n**Critical Dependencies**:\n- `runtimeService` (internalapi.RuntimeService): gRPC client to actual container runtime\n- `imageService` (internalapi.ImageManagerService): gRPC client for image operations\n- `containerManager` (cm.ContainerManager): Resource allocation and cgroup enforcement\n- `livenessManager`, `readinessManager`, `startupManager`: Probe result caching\n- `containerGC`: Garbage collector for dead containers\n- `internalLifecycle` (cm.InternalContainerLifecycle): Resource manager hooks\n\n**Key Responsibilities**:\n1. Translate Kubernetes pod specs to CRI sandbox/container configurations\n2. Orchestrate container creation sequence (init → app → ephemeral)\n3. Manage image pulling with credential providers and retry logic\n4. Coordinate with resource managers for CPU, memory, device allocation\n5. Track container state transitions and generate events\n\n### 2.3 Resource Management Layer\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/cm/container_manager.go`\n\nThe `ContainerManager` interface defines the resource enforcement subsystem:\n\n```\nContainerManager interface manages:\n- System cgroups (kubelet, runtime, system processes)\n- Pod-level cgroups and QoS hierarchies\n- CPU allocation policies (none, static)\n- Memory management and reservation\n- Device plugin resources\n- Topology-aware resource placement\n- Pod admission and pre-flight checks\n- Node allocatable enforcement\n```\n\n**Linux Implementation**: `pkg/kubelet/cm/container_manager_linux.go`\n\nCreates and manages three cgroup hierarchies:\n\n1. **System Cgroups**: Container for kubelet/runtime processes\n2. **Kubelet Cgroups**: Limits on kubelet itself\n3. **Pod Cgroups**: Hierarchy where each pod gets a cgroup based on QoS class\n   - `kubepods` (burstable) → pod cgroup → container cgroups\n   - `kubepods-burstable` (burstable) → pod cgroup → container cgroups\n   - `kubepods-besteffort` (besteffort) → pod cgroup → container cgroups\n\n### 2.4 Pod Container Manager\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/cm/pod_container_manager_linux.go`\n\nManages pod-level cgroup creation and resource configuration:\n\n**Responsibilities**:\n- Create pod-level cgroup if QoS cgroup hierarchy is enabled\n- Apply pod-level CPU and memory limits before container creation\n- Handle MemoryQoS settings (cgroup v2 memory throttling)\n- Enforce PID limits at pod scope\n- Update pod resource configuration dynamically\n\n### 2.5 Resource Managers (Pluggable)\n\nThe container manager coordinates with specialized resource managers through `InternalContainerLifecycle` hooks:\n\n**pkg/kubelet/cm/cpumanager**: CPU affinity and exclusive CPU allocation\n- Policies: `none` (no affinity), `static` (pinning for Guaranteed pods)\n- Topology-aware placement via NodeInfo topology\n\n**pkg/kubelet/cm/memorymanager**: Memory affinity for NUMA systems\n- Memory allocation based on topology hints\n- Manages memory reservation per pod\n\n**pkg/kubelet/cm/devicemanager**: Device plugin resource allocation\n- Tracks device availability from plugins\n- Allocates devices to containers via mounts and environment\n\n**pkg/kubelet/cm/topologymanager**: Coordinate CPU, memory, and device allocation\n- Collects topology hints from all managers\n- Ensures aligned allocation or fails pod admission\n\n## 3. End-to-End Control and Request Flow\n\n### 3.1 Pod Synchronization Flow (Main Orchestration)\n\n**Entry Point**: `kubeGenericRuntimeManager.SyncPod()` in `pkg/kubelet/kuberuntime/kuberuntime_manager.go:1394`\n\n```\nSyncPod orchestrates 8 sequential steps:\n\nStep 1: Compute Sandbox and Container Changes\n├─ Call computePodActions(pod, podStatus, restartAllContainers)\n├─ Determine what needs killing, creating, or resizing\n└─ Identify sandbox changes (network, security context)\n\nStep 2: Kill Pod Sandbox if Necessary\n├─ If sandbox changed: stop all containers and remove sandbox\n└─ Purge init containers for clean restart\n\nStep 3: Kill Unwanted Containers\n├─ For each container marked for termination\n├─ Call killContainer(pod, containerID, gracePeriod)\n└─ Handle in-place restart container removals\n\nStep 4: Create Pod Sandbox\n├─ generatePodSandboxConfig(pod) → CRI config\n│  ├─ Metadata (name, UID, attempt count)\n│  ├─ DNS configuration\n│  ├─ Network namespace mode (host/container/none)\n│  ├─ Security context (SELinux, AppArmor, seccomp)\n│  └─ RuntimeClass handler lookup\n├─ Call runtimeService.RunPodSandbox(config, handler)\n└─ Store returned sandboxID for containers\n\nStep 5: Create Ephemeral Containers\n├─ For each ephemeral container spec\n└─ createContainer() with copy-on-write semantics\n\nStep 6: Create Init Containers\n├─ Containers execute in order (must complete)\n├─ Each init container waits for previous one\n└─ Handles init container restarts\n\nStep 7: Resize Running Containers (if InPlacePodVerticalScaling)\n├─ For Guaranteed QoS pods\n├─ Call runtimeService.RespawnContainer()\n└─ Adjust CPU/memory allocations\n\nStep 8: Create Normal Application Containers\n├─ Containers run in parallel (optional)\n├─ generateContainerConfig() for each container\n├─ Call createContainer() for each\n└─ Start all containers\n```\n\n### 3.2 Container Creation Sequence\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/kuberuntime/kuberuntime_container.go`\n\nFor each container, the flow is:\n\n```\ncreateContainer(ctx, pod, container):\n  1. generateContainerConfig(pod, container)\n     ├─ Image with runtime handler (e.g., nvidia for GPU)\n     ├─ Command, args, env variables\n     ├─ Mounts (volumes, tmpfs, secrets)\n     ├─ Devices (block, character, CDI devices)\n     ├─ Port mappings\n     └─ Security context (capabilities, SELinux, user namespace)\n\n  2. Lifecycle hook: PreCreateContainer()\n     ├─ Call containerManager.InternalContainerLifecycle\n     └─ (CPU/memory manager setup, topology hints)\n\n  3. Ensure pod container exists (pod cgroup)\n     └─ containerManager.NewPodContainerManager().EnsureExists()\n\n  4. Call runtimeService.CreateContainer(sandboxID, containerConfig)\n     └─ Runtime creates container (not started yet)\n\n  5. Lifecycle hook: PreStartContainer()\n     ├─ CPU manager: AddContainer(pod, container, containerID)\n     ├─ Memory manager: AddContainer(pod, container, containerID)\n     └─ Topology manager: AddContainer(pod, container, containerID)\n\n  6. Call runtimeService.StartContainer(containerID)\n     └─ Container starts executing\n```\n\n### 3.3 Pod Status Query Flow\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/kuberuntime/kuberuntime_manager.go`\n\nCalled by kubelet's status manager to fetch current pod state:\n\n```\nGetPodStatus(ctx, uid, name, namespace):\n  1. Call runtimeService.PodSandboxStatus(sandboxID)\n  2. For each container in pod:\n     └─ Call runtimeService.ContainerStatus(containerID)\n  3. Extract status fields:\n     ├─ State (created, running, exited)\n     ├─ Creation/start/finish times\n     ├─ Exit code and reason\n     ├─ Restart count\n     ├─ Resource usage (if available)\n     └─ Mount information\n  4. Aggregate into PodStatus{\n       IPs, ContainerStatuses[], SandboxStatuses[]\n     }\n```\n\n### 3.4 Garbage Collection Flow\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/kuberuntime/kuberuntime_gc.go`\n\nRuns periodically to clean up dead containers:\n\n```\nGarbageCollect(ctx, gcPolicy, allSourcesReady, evictNonDeletedPods):\n  1. List all containers from runtime\n  2. Group containers by (pod UID, container name)\n  3. For each group, keep only N most recent containers per policy:\n     ├─ maxPerPodContainer (default 5)\n     ├─ maxContainers (default 100)\n     └─ minAge (default 1 minute)\n  4. Mark excess containers for removal\n  5. If allSourcesReady:\n     ├─ Also GC containers from unknown pods\n     └─ (pods not in current pod list)\n  6. Stop containers before removal (with grace period)\n  7. Call runtimeService.RemoveContainer(containerID)\n  8. Similarly GC sandboxes (but less aggressive)\n```\n\n## 4. Failure Modes, Limits, and Design Tradeoffs\n\n### 4.1 Container Lifecycle Failures\n\n**Sandbox Creation Failures**\n- **Cause**: Network namespace creation fails, SELinux context invalid, resource quota exceeded\n- **Behavior**: Pod marked as failed, containers never created\n- **Recovery**: Retry mechanism with exponential backoff, user must inspect logs and fix spec\n\n**Container Creation Timeouts**\n- **Cause**: Image pull hangs (network), runtime unresponsive\n- **Behavior**: Timeout after default 5 minutes, pod in pending state\n- **Recovery**: Event recorded, retry on next sync cycle\n\n**Resource Allocation Failures**\n- **Cause**: Insufficient CPUs for static affinity, devices unavailable, memory quota exceeded\n- **Behavior**: Pod admission rejected before containers created\n- **Recovery**: Requires resource freeing (other pod eviction) or reducing request\n\n**Container Startup Failures**\n- **Cause**: Application crashed immediately, bad command, permission denied\n- **Behavior**: Container exits quickly, restart policy determines action\n- **Recovery**: Restart loop with exponential backoff (RestartPolicy)\n\n### 4.2 Operational Limits\n\n**Container Count Per Pod**\n- No hard limit in kubelet\n- Practical limit: ~100 containers per pod (scheduling, network overhead)\n- Cgroup management overhead increases linearly\n\n**Container Image Sizes**\n- Runtime-dependent, typically limited by node disk\n- Large images cause longer pull times, delays pod startup\n- Multiple images on same layer share storage (deduplication depends on runtime)\n\n**Grace Period for Shutdown**\n- Default: 30 seconds per pod\n- Minimum: 2 seconds (hardcoded in kuberuntime)\n- Enforced by kubelet + runtime; hard kill (SIGKILL) after grace expires\n\n**Cgroup Resource Limits**\n- CPU shares: integer 2-262144, no unit enforced by cgroup\n- Memory: bytes only, no swap separate from memory (cgroup v1 vs v2 difference)\n- CPU quota: enforces hard limit if cpuCFSQuota enabled\n\n### 4.3 Key Design Tradeoffs\n\n**Sandbox-per-Pod Architecture**\n- **Tradeoff**: One network namespace per pod vs. per-container networking\n- **Rationale**: Simplifies pod networking (all containers share pod IP), matches Kubernetes model\n- **Cost**: Cannot change pod network isolation after creation; sandbox recreation needed\n\n**Preemptive Kill vs. Graceful Termination**\n- **Tradeoff**: Always allow grace period vs. fast cleanup\n- **Rationale**: Respect application shutdown hooks (SIGTERM → graceful, SIGKILL after)\n- **Cost**: Delayed eviction; can be overridden for node pressure scenarios\n\n**Pod Cgroup Hierarchy**\n- **Tradeoff**: Pod-level cgroup limits vs. per-container granularity\n- **Rationale**: Enforce minimum QoS guarantees at pod scope; QoS class coordination\n- **Cost**: Cannot exceed pod limit even if individual containers idle; less fine-grained control\n\n**Stateless Container Manager**\n- **Tradeoff**: Recompute state every sync cycle vs. in-memory tracking\n- **Rationale**: Resilient to kubelet restarts, always consistent with runtime ground truth\n- **Cost**: Repeated runtime queries; overhead for large pod counts\n\n**ImagePullBackOff vs. Error Distinction**\n- **Tradeoff**: Retry image pulls indefinitely vs. fail fast on bad image\n- **Rationale**: Network transients are common; bad images should retry (user may push fix)\n- **Cost**: User confusion on actually bad images (typos in name, auth failures)\n\n## 5. Extension Points, Customization Hooks, and Associated Risks\n\n### 5.1 Internal Container Lifecycle Hooks\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/cm/internal_container_lifecycle.go`\n\nThree extension points during container creation:\n\n```go\ntype InternalContainerLifecycle interface {\n    // Called after container config generated, before CRI CreateContainer\n    PreCreateContainer(pod, container, containerConfig)\n\n    // Called after runtimeService.StartContainer\n    PreStartContainer(pod, container, containerID)\n\n    // Called after container stopped, before removal\n    PostStopContainer(containerID)\n}\n```\n\n**Current Implementations**:\n- **CPU Manager** (`PreStartContainer`): Pins CPUs for Guaranteed pods\n- **Memory Manager** (`PreStartContainer`): Applies memory affinity\n- **Topology Manager** (`PreStartContainer` and `PostStopContainer`): Coordinates all managers\n\n**Risk**: Bugs in custom hooks block container startup; pod creation hangs if hook panics\n\n### 5.2 RuntimeClass Handler Selection\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/kuberuntime/kuberuntime_sandbox.go:58`\n\nAllows selecting alternative runtime handlers per pod:\n\n```go\nruntimeHandler, err := m.runtimeClassManager.LookupRuntimeHandler(pod.Spec.RuntimeClassName)\n// Passed to runtimeService.RunPodSandbox(..., runtimeHandler)\n```\n\n**Use Cases**:\n- GPU workloads (nvidia, amd handlers)\n- Kata Containers for isolation\n- Custom runtime configurations\n\n**Risk**: Handler misconfiguration silently falls back to default; wrong isolation if handler unavailable\n\n### 5.3 Device Plugin Integration\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/cm/device_manager`\n\nDevice managers expose custom resources:\n\n```go\ncontainerManager.UpdatePluginResources(nodeInfo, podAdmitAttributes)\n// Returns allocated devices via:\ncontainerManager.GetResources(ctx, pod, container)\n```\n\n**Extension Pattern**:\n1. Plugin registers via kubelet plugin watcher\n2. Device manager discovers available resources\n3. On pod admission: topology manager allocates devices\n4. On container creation: GetResources returns mounts, env vars, annotations\n\n**Risk**: Plugin not responding causes pod admission hangs; resource leaks if plugin crashes\n\n### 5.4 Pre-Admit Handlers\n\n**File**: Referenced by `containerManager.GetAllocateResourcesPodAdmitHandler()`\n\nPods are admitted before scheduling (kubelet-local gate):\n\n```\nPodAdmitHandler is called:\n  Before any containers created\n  With pod and all current active pods\n  Can reject pod or modify resource allocations\n```\n\n**Use Cases**:\n- Custom admission control (security policies)\n- Dynamic capacity tracking beyond kubelet-reported\n- Pod resource overcommit enforcement\n\n**Risk**: Rejecting pods here bypasses API server validation; inconsistent with scheduler decisions\n\n### 5.5 Image Credential Providers\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/images/pullmanager`\n\nCustom executables can provide registry credentials:\n\n```\nCredential Provider Plugin:\n  Input: image reference\n  Output: username/password or bearer token\n  Executed as subprocess during image pull\n  Can be slow (synchronous in critical path)\n```\n\n**Risk**: Slow credential providers delay all pod startup; credential exposure in logs if not careful\n\n## 6. Operational Implications\n\n### 6.1 Performance Characteristics\n\n**Container Startup Latency**\n- Sandbox creation: 10-50ms (depends on network namespace setup, cgroup creation)\n- Image pull: seconds to minutes (network bound, registry latency)\n- Container creation: 50-200ms per container (runtime-dependent)\n- Container start: 0-5000ms (application dependent, health probe startup)\n- **Total first pod**: seconds to minutes (dominated by image pull)\n- **Total subsequent pod** (cached image): hundreds of milliseconds\n\n**Resource Manager Overhead**\n- CPU manager affinity computation: O(n) where n = CPU count\n- Topology manager hint collection: 3-5ms per container (gathers from CPU/memory/device managers)\n- Cgroup creation: ~1-10ms per pod (filesystem operations)\n- **Scaling limit**: ~100 containers on single node before noticeable delay\n\n**Garbage Collection Overhead**\n- Lists all containers from runtime: O(n) where n = total containers ever created\n- Typical: <100ms for nodes with ~100 total containers\n- **Risk**: GC pause blocks pod operations if many dead containers\n\n### 6.2 Reliability and Resilience\n\n**Recovery from Kubelet Restart**\n- On restart, kubelet queries runtime for all pods and containers\n- Containers continue running under runtime; kubelet re-syncs state\n- **Safe**: Long-running pods unaffected; state consistent from runtime ground truth\n- **Risk**: If container runtime crashed, kubelet cannot detect state; assumes runtime recovery\n\n**Handling Hung Container Runtime**\n- All CRI calls timeout after default 2 minutes\n- Timeout causes pod to be marked failed; events recorded\n- **Limitation**: Cannot forcefully kill runtime; depends on external monitoring\n\n**Orphaned Containers**\n- If kubelet crashes, containers keep running\n- Upon restart: kubelet reattaches via container ID matching\n- **Risk**: If containers deleted from runtime externally, kubelet has stale state (pod never terminates)\n\n### 6.3 Resource Accounting and Limits\n\n**Memory Pressure Handling**\n- Node eviction manager uses cgroup memory.max + usage to trigger eviction\n- Container manager enforces memory.max at pod scope\n- **Limitation**: No memory overcommit enforcement; OOMKiller is last resort\n- **Tuning**: `--memory-manager-policy=static` for guaranteed allocations\n\n**CPU Throttling**\n- CPU limits enforced via cpu.cfs_quota_us / cpu.cfs_period_us\n- CPU manager reserves CPUs for Guaranteed pods (no sharing)\n- **Limitation**: Noisy neighbor problem if bursting pods on same node\n- **Tuning**: `--cpu-manager-policy=static` + CPU topology hints\n\n**Disk I/O Limits**\n- Not enforced by container manager (delegated to runtime/systemd)\n- Cleanup policy for images/logs relies on simple age/count heuristics\n- **Risk**: Slow disk I/O can stall pod startup\n\n### 6.4 Observability and Debugging\n\n**Event Recording**\n- Pod events recorded on key transitions (SandboxChanged, ContainerBackOff, etc.)\n- Events help operators understand failure reasons\n- **Limitation**: No structured logs of internal decisions; must read kubelet logs\n\n**Metrics Exported**\n- `kubelet_pod_worker_duration_seconds`: Time to sync pod\n- `kubelet_container_manager_*`: Resource manager metrics (CPU affinity, etc.)\n- `kubelet_runtime_crio_operations_duration_seconds`: CRI call latencies (per operation type)\n- **Gap**: No metrics on sandbox/container state mismatches\n\n**Pod Log Access**\n- Kubelet reads container logs from runtime via GetContainerLogs\n- Supports `--tail` and `--follow` for streaming\n- **Limitation**: Logs lost if container removed before access\n\n### 6.5 Platform-Specific Behaviors\n\n**Linux-Specific**\n- Cgroup v1 (deprecated) vs. v2 (recommended) have different resource enforcement\n- User namespaces supported (UserNamespacesSupport feature gate)\n- Shared PID namespace across pod supported (ShareProcessNamespace)\n- **Deprecation Warning**: cgroup v1 usage logged; migration path to v2 required\n\n**Windows-Specific** (File: `pkg/kubelet/cm/container_manager_windows.go`)\n- No cgroup support; uses Windows Job Objects for resource limits\n- Memory limits via Job Object limits\n- CPU shares via Job scheduling (no precise pinning like Linux)\n- Network isolation via HNS (Host Networking Service)\n- **Limitation**: No fine-grained CPU affinity; device management different\n\n**macOS (Not Supported for Production)**\n- Minimal runtime support; used for local development only\n- No container isolation (containers run as local processes)\n- Not tested in CI; not recommended for real workloads\n\n## 7. File and Directory Map\n\n### Core Runtime Interfaces\n- `pkg/kubelet/container/runtime.go` (450 lines)\n  - `Runtime` interface: SyncPod, KillPod, GarbageCollect, image operations\n  - Container/Pod/Status type definitions\n  - GC policy, port mapping, mount, device types\n\n- `pkg/kubelet/container/container_gc.go` (50 lines)\n  - Wrapper around Runtime.GarbageCollect with policies\n\n### Runtime Implementation\n- `pkg/kubelet/kuberuntime/` (30+ files)\n  - `kuberuntime_manager.go` (1500+ lines): Main orchestration, SyncPod logic\n  - `kuberuntime_container.go` (500+ lines): Container creation, config generation\n  - `kuberuntime_sandbox.go` (400+ lines): Pod sandbox creation, network setup\n  - `kuberuntime_gc.go` (400+ lines): Garbage collector for containers/sandboxes\n  - `kuberuntime_image.go`: Image pull, list, remove operations\n  - `instrumented_services.go`: Metrics/tracing wrapper around CRI calls\n\n### Resource Management\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/container_manager.go` (320 lines)\n  - `ContainerManager` interface: Start, GetResources, UpdateQOSCgroups, etc.\n\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/container_manager_linux.go` (1000+ lines)\n  - `containerManagerImpl` struct: Cgroup management, system containers\n  - `NewContainerManager`: Factory with cadvisor, subsystem detection\n\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/pod_container_manager_linux.go` (300+ lines)\n  - `PodContainerManager` interface: Exists, EnsureExists, Destroy\n  - Pod-level cgroup setup, QoS hierarchy\n\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/qos_container_manager_linux.go` (300+ lines)\n  - Top-level QoS cgroup management (guaranteed, burstable, besteffort)\n\n### Resource Managers (Pluggable)\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/cpumanager/` (500+ lines)\n  - `Manager` interface: AddContainer, RemoveContainer, Status\n  - Policies: none, static\n  - State: assignment tracking per pod/container\n\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/memorymanager/` (400+ lines)\n  - Similar structure to CPU manager\n  - Memory affinity for NUMA\n\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/devicemanager/` (300+ lines)\n  - Device plugin discovery and allocation\n\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/topologymanager/` (400+ lines)\n  - Topology hints coordination between managers\n  - Hint aggregation and conflict resolution\n\n### Lifecycle Hooks\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/internal_container_lifecycle.go` (60 lines)\n  - `InternalContainerLifecycle` interface: PreCreateContainer, PreStartContainer, PostStopContainer\n  - Implementation coordinating CPU/memory/topology managers\n\n### Supporting Types\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/types.go`: CgroupName, CgroupManager interface, Status types\n- `pkg/kubelet/container/cache.go`: Container hash caching for update detection\n- `pkg/kubelet/container/sync_result.go`: SyncResult aggregation for pod operations\n\n## 8. Summary: Critical Invariants and Design Principles\n\n### Invariants\n1. **One Sandbox Per Pod**: Each pod has exactly one sandbox (network namespace) that all containers share\n2. **Container Order Matters**: Init containers must complete before app containers start; ephemeral containers anytime\n3. **State Convergence**: SyncPod drives pod toward desired state through repeated calls; idempotent\n4. **Cgroup Hierarchy**: Pod cgroups nested under QoS cgroups nested under kubelet cgroup\n5. **GC Safety**: Dead containers only removed if age > minAge AND not from untracked pods (unless forced)\n\n### Design Principles\n1. **Stateless Manager**: Kubelet container manager queries runtime ground truth; no in-memory pod state\n2. **Graceful Termination**: Respect application shutdown; escalate to SIGKILL only after grace period\n3. **Resource Isolation**: QoS guarantees enforced via cgroups; no application-level throttling\n4. **Pluggable Resources**: CPU/memory/device managers plugged via hooks; topology coordinated\n5. **Progressive Pod Lifecycle**: Containers created sequentially per dependencies; not all-or-nothing\n6. **Observability**: Events and metrics on key transitions; ground truth from container runtime\n\nThese principles and invariants ensure Kubernetes pods behave predictably across diverse node configurations and container runtimes while maintaining isolation and resource fairness.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":12,"cache_creation_input_tokens":7342,"cache_read_input_tokens":79775,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":7342},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"22b56103-978b-49c0-b2f6-073ea5c7c4a4","uuid":"f7fe0e72-eb90-48fa-b6fb-955a05e06390"}

Subsystem Purpose and Boundaries\n\nThe kubelet container manager subsystem is responsible for managing the complete lifecycle of containers and pods on a Kubernetes node. It serves as the critical interface between the Kubernetes control plane's desired pod specifications and the actual container runtime (Docker, containerd, CRI-O, etc.) through the Container Runtime Interface (CRI).\n\n### Purpose\n\n- **Container Lifecycle Management**: Create, start, stop, and delete containers with proper ordering and resource allocation\n- **Resource Enforcement**: Apply CPU limits, memory reservations, and device allocations through cgroups\n- **Pod Orchestration**: Coordinate sandbox creation and container dependencies (init containers, ephemeral containers, app containers)\n- **State Reconciliation**: Drive containers toward desired state through repeated synchronization cycles\n- **Garbage Collection**: Remove unused containers and images to prevent resource exhaustion\n\n### Boundaries\n\n**Upstream Dependencies**:\n- **Pod Specifications** (`v1.Pod`): Desired pod configuration from API server\n- **Pod Status**: Current runtime state queried from container runtime\n- **Configuration Sources**: File-based manifests, API server, HTTP endpoints\n- **Resource Allocations**: From device plugins, CPU/memory managers, and topology managers\n\n**Downstream Dependencies**:\n- **Container Runtime Interface (CRI)**: gRPC service for pod/container operations\n- **Cgroup Managers** (Linux): Apply resource limits via cgroup filesystems\n- **Volume Mounts**: Storage subsystem for ephemeral and persistent volumes\n- **Network Plugins**: CNI for pod networking (handled separately in kubelet)\n\n**External Services**:\n- Image registry access for container image pulls\n- Kubelet's own node status and eviction managers\n- Pod lifecycle hooks (pre-start, post-stop handlers)\n\n## 2. Key Components and Responsibility Split\n\n### 2.1 Runtime Interface Layer\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/container/runtime.go`\n\nThe `Runtime` interface defines the contract that all container runtimes must implement:\n\n```\nRuntime interface provides:\n- SyncPod(ctx, pod, podStatus, pullSecrets, backOff, restartAllContainers) → PodSyncResult\n  Orchestrates pod creation/updates, the primary entry point\n\n- KillPod(ctx, pod, runningPod, gracePeriodOverride) → error\n  Terminates all containers in a pod with grace period\n\n- GetPodStatus(ctx, uid, name, namespace) → *PodStatus\n  Queries runtime for current pod state\n\n- GarbageCollect(ctx, gcPolicy, allSourcesReady, evictNonDeletedPods) → error\n  Cleans up dead containers per policy\n\n- ImageService: PullImage, ListImages, RemoveImage operations\n```\n\nThis interface allows multiple runtime implementations to be plugged in, though the dominant implementation is `kubeGenericRuntimeManager`.\n\n### 2.2 Concrete Runtime Manager\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/kuberuntime/kuberuntime_manager.go`\n\n`kubeGenericRuntimeManager` is the main orchestration component. It holds:\n\n**Critical Dependencies**:\n- `runtimeService` (internalapi.RuntimeService): gRPC client to actual container runtime\n- `imageService` (internalapi.ImageManagerService): gRPC client for image operations\n- `containerManager` (cm.ContainerManager): Resource allocation and cgroup enforcement\n- `livenessManager`, `readinessManager`, `startupManager`: Probe result caching\n- `containerGC`: Garbage collector for dead containers\n- `internalLifecycle` (cm.InternalContainerLifecycle): Resource manager hooks\n\n**Key Responsibilities**:\n1. Translate Kubernetes pod specs to CRI sandbox/container configurations\n2. Orchestrate container creation sequence (init → app → ephemeral)\n3. Manage image pulling with credential providers and retry logic\n4. Coordinate with resource managers for CPU, memory, device allocation\n5. Track container state transitions and generate events\n\n### 2.3 Resource Management Layer\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/cm/container_manager.go`\n\nThe `ContainerManager` interface defines the resource enforcement subsystem:\n\n```\nContainerManager interface manages:\n- System cgroups (kubelet, runtime, system processes)\n- Pod-level cgroups and QoS hierarchies\n- CPU allocation policies (none, static)\n- Memory management and reservation\n- Device plugin resources\n- Topology-aware resource placement\n- Pod admission and pre-flight checks\n- Node allocatable enforcement\n```\n\n**Linux Implementation**: `pkg/kubelet/cm/container_manager_linux.go`\n\nCreates and manages three cgroup hierarchies:\n\n1. **System Cgroups**: Container for kubelet/runtime processes\n2. **Kubelet Cgroups**: Limits on kubelet itself\n3. **Pod Cgroups**: Hierarchy where each pod gets a cgroup based on QoS class\n   - `kubepods` (burstable) → pod cgroup → container cgroups\n   - `kubepods-burstable` (burstable) → pod cgroup → container cgroups\n   - `kubepods-besteffort` (besteffort) → pod cgroup → container cgroups\n\n### 2.4 Pod Container Manager\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/cm/pod_container_manager_linux.go`\n\nManages pod-level cgroup creation and resource configuration:\n\n**Responsibilities**:\n- Create pod-level cgroup if QoS cgroup hierarchy is enabled\n- Apply pod-level CPU and memory limits before container creation\n- Handle MemoryQoS settings (cgroup v2 memory throttling)\n- Enforce PID limits at pod scope\n- Update pod resource configuration dynamically\n\n### 2.5 Resource Managers (Pluggable)\n\nThe container manager coordinates with specialized resource managers through `InternalContainerLifecycle` hooks:\n\n**pkg/kubelet/cm/cpumanager**: CPU affinity and exclusive CPU allocation\n- Policies: `none` (no affinity), `static` (pinning for Guaranteed pods)\n- Topology-aware placement via NodeInfo topology\n\n**pkg/kubelet/cm/memorymanager**: Memory affinity for NUMA systems\n- Memory allocation based on topology hints\n- Manages memory reservation per pod\n\n**pkg/kubelet/cm/devicemanager**: Device plugin resource allocation\n- Tracks device availability from plugins\n- Allocates devices to containers via mounts and environment\n\n**pkg/kubelet/cm/topologymanager**: Coordinate CPU, memory, and device allocation\n- Collects topology hints from all managers\n- Ensures aligned allocation or fails pod admission\n\n## 3. End-to-End Control and Request Flow\n\n### 3.1 Pod Synchronization Flow (Main Orchestration)\n\n**Entry Point**: `kubeGenericRuntimeManager.SyncPod()` in `pkg/kubelet/kuberuntime/kuberuntime_manager.go:1394`\n\n```\nSyncPod orchestrates 8 sequential steps:\n\nStep 1: Compute Sandbox and Container Changes\n├─ Call computePodActions(pod, podStatus, restartAllContainers)\n├─ Determine what needs killing, creating, or resizing\n└─ Identify sandbox changes (network, security context)\n\nStep 2: Kill Pod Sandbox if Necessary\n├─ If sandbox changed: stop all containers and remove sandbox\n└─ Purge init containers for clean restart\n\nStep 3: Kill Unwanted Containers\n├─ For each container marked for termination\n├─ Call killContainer(pod, containerID, gracePeriod)\n└─ Handle in-place restart container removals\n\nStep 4: Create Pod Sandbox\n├─ generatePodSandboxConfig(pod) → CRI config\n│  ├─ Metadata (name, UID, attempt count)\n│  ├─ DNS configuration\n│  ├─ Network namespace mode (host/container/none)\n│  ├─ Security context (SELinux, AppArmor, seccomp)\n│  └─ RuntimeClass handler lookup\n├─ Call runtimeService.RunPodSandbox(config, handler)\n└─ Store returned sandboxID for containers\n\nStep 5: Create Ephemeral Containers\n├─ For each ephemeral container spec\n└─ createContainer() with copy-on-write semantics\n\nStep 6: Create Init Containers\n├─ Containers execute in order (must complete)\n├─ Each init container waits for previous one\n└─ Handles init container restarts\n\nStep 7: Resize Running Containers (if InPlacePodVerticalScaling)\n├─ For Guaranteed QoS pods\n├─ Call runtimeService.RespawnContainer()\n└─ Adjust CPU/memory allocations\n\nStep 8: Create Normal Application Containers\n├─ Containers run in parallel (optional)\n├─ generateContainerConfig() for each container\n├─ Call createContainer() for each\n└─ Start all containers\n```\n\n### 3.2 Container Creation Sequence\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/kuberuntime/kuberuntime_container.go`\n\nFor each container, the flow is:\n\n```\ncreateContainer(ctx, pod, container):\n  1. generateContainerConfig(pod, container)\n     ├─ Image with runtime handler (e.g., nvidia for GPU)\n     ├─ Command, args, env variables\n     ├─ Mounts (volumes, tmpfs, secrets)\n     ├─ Devices (block, character, CDI devices)\n     ├─ Port mappings\n     └─ Security context (capabilities, SELinux, user namespace)\n\n  2. Lifecycle hook: PreCreateContainer()\n     ├─ Call containerManager.InternalContainerLifecycle\n     └─ (CPU/memory manager setup, topology hints)\n\n  3. Ensure pod container exists (pod cgroup)\n     └─ containerManager.NewPodContainerManager().EnsureExists()\n\n  4. Call runtimeService.CreateContainer(sandboxID, containerConfig)\n     └─ Runtime creates container (not started yet)\n\n  5. Lifecycle hook: PreStartContainer()\n     ├─ CPU manager: AddContainer(pod, container, containerID)\n     ├─ Memory manager: AddContainer(pod, container, containerID)\n     └─ Topology manager: AddContainer(pod, container, containerID)\n\n  6. Call runtimeService.StartContainer(containerID)\n     └─ Container starts executing\n```\n\n### 3.3 Pod Status Query Flow\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/kuberuntime/kuberuntime_manager.go`\n\nCalled by kubelet's status manager to fetch current pod state:\n\n```\nGetPodStatus(ctx, uid, name, namespace):\n  1. Call runtimeService.PodSandboxStatus(sandboxID)\n  2. For each container in pod:\n     └─ Call runtimeService.ContainerStatus(containerID)\n  3. Extract status fields:\n     ├─ State (created, running, exited)\n     ├─ Creation/start/finish times\n     ├─ Exit code and reason\n     ├─ Restart count\n     ├─ Resource usage (if available)\n     └─ Mount information\n  4. Aggregate into PodStatus{\n       IPs, ContainerStatuses[], SandboxStatuses[]\n     }\n```\n\n### 3.4 Garbage Collection Flow\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/kuberuntime/kuberuntime_gc.go`\n\nRuns periodically to clean up dead containers:\n\n```\nGarbageCollect(ctx, gcPolicy, allSourcesReady, evictNonDeletedPods):\n  1. List all containers from runtime\n  2. Group containers by (pod UID, container name)\n  3. For each group, keep only N most recent containers per policy:\n     ├─ maxPerPodContainer (default 5)\n     ├─ maxContainers (default 100)\n     └─ minAge (default 1 minute)\n  4. Mark excess containers for removal\n  5. If allSourcesReady:\n     ├─ Also GC containers from unknown pods\n     └─ (pods not in current pod list)\n  6. Stop containers before removal (with grace period)\n  7. Call runtimeService.RemoveContainer(containerID)\n  8. Similarly GC sandboxes (but less aggressive)\n```\n\n## 4. Failure Modes, Limits, and Design Tradeoffs\n\n### 4.1 Container Lifecycle Failures\n\n**Sandbox Creation Failures**\n- **Cause**: Network namespace creation fails, SELinux context invalid, resource quota exceeded\n- **Behavior**: Pod marked as failed, containers never created\n- **Recovery**: Retry mechanism with exponential backoff, user must inspect logs and fix spec\n\n**Container Creation Timeouts**\n- **Cause**: Image pull hangs (network), runtime unresponsive\n- **Behavior**: Timeout after default 5 minutes, pod in pending state\n- **Recovery**: Event recorded, retry on next sync cycle\n\n**Resource Allocation Failures**\n- **Cause**: Insufficient CPUs for static affinity, devices unavailable, memory quota exceeded\n- **Behavior**: Pod admission rejected before containers created\n- **Recovery**: Requires resource freeing (other pod eviction) or reducing request\n\n**Container Startup Failures**\n- **Cause**: Application crashed immediately, bad command, permission denied\n- **Behavior**: Container exits quickly, restart policy determines action\n- **Recovery**: Restart loop with exponential backoff (RestartPolicy)\n\n### 4.2 Operational Limits\n\n**Container Count Per Pod**\n- No hard limit in kubelet\n- Practical limit: ~100 containers per pod (scheduling, network overhead)\n- Cgroup management overhead increases linearly\n\n**Container Image Sizes**\n- Runtime-dependent, typically limited by node disk\n- Large images cause longer pull times, delays pod startup\n- Multiple images on same layer share storage (deduplication depends on runtime)\n\n**Grace Period for Shutdown**\n- Default: 30 seconds per pod\n- Minimum: 2 seconds (hardcoded in kuberuntime)\n- Enforced by kubelet + runtime; hard kill (SIGKILL) after grace expires\n\n**Cgroup Resource Limits**\n- CPU shares: integer 2-262144, no unit enforced by cgroup\n- Memory: bytes only, no swap separate from memory (cgroup v1 vs v2 difference)\n- CPU quota: enforces hard limit if cpuCFSQuota enabled\n\n### 4.3 Key Design Tradeoffs\n\n**Sandbox-per-Pod Architecture**\n- **Tradeoff**: One network namespace per pod vs. per-container networking\n- **Rationale**: Simplifies pod networking (all containers share pod IP), matches Kubernetes model\n- **Cost**: Cannot change pod network isolation after creation; sandbox recreation needed\n\n**Preemptive Kill vs. Graceful Termination**\n- **Tradeoff**: Always allow grace period vs. fast cleanup\n- **Rationale**: Respect application shutdown hooks (SIGTERM → graceful, SIGKILL after)\n- **Cost**: Delayed eviction; can be overridden for node pressure scenarios\n\n**Pod Cgroup Hierarchy**\n- **Tradeoff**: Pod-level cgroup limits vs. per-container granularity\n- **Rationale**: Enforce minimum QoS guarantees at pod scope; QoS class coordination\n- **Cost**: Cannot exceed pod limit even if individual containers idle; less fine-grained control\n\n**Stateless Container Manager**\n- **Tradeoff**: Recompute state every sync cycle vs. in-memory tracking\n- **Rationale**: Resilient to kubelet restarts, always consistent with runtime ground truth\n- **Cost**: Repeated runtime queries; overhead for large pod counts\n\n**ImagePullBackOff vs. Error Distinction**\n- **Tradeoff**: Retry image pulls indefinitely vs. fail fast on bad image\n- **Rationale**: Network transients are common; bad images should retry (user may push fix)\n- **Cost**: User confusion on actually bad images (typos in name, auth failures)\n\n## 5. Extension Points, Customization Hooks, and Associated Risks\n\n### 5.1 Internal Container Lifecycle Hooks\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/cm/internal_container_lifecycle.go`\n\nThree extension points during container creation:\n\n```go\ntype InternalContainerLifecycle interface {\n    // Called after container config generated, before CRI CreateContainer\n    PreCreateContainer(pod, container, containerConfig)\n\n    // Called after runtimeService.StartContainer\n    PreStartContainer(pod, container, containerID)\n\n    // Called after container stopped, before removal\n    PostStopContainer(containerID)\n}\n```\n\n**Current Implementations**:\n- **CPU Manager** (`PreStartContainer`): Pins CPUs for Guaranteed pods\n- **Memory Manager** (`PreStartContainer`): Applies memory affinity\n- **Topology Manager** (`PreStartContainer` and `PostStopContainer`): Coordinates all managers\n\n**Risk**: Bugs in custom hooks block container startup; pod creation hangs if hook panics\n\n### 5.2 RuntimeClass Handler Selection\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/kuberuntime/kuberuntime_sandbox.go:58`\n\nAllows selecting alternative runtime handlers per pod:\n\n```go\nruntimeHandler, err := m.runtimeClassManager.LookupRuntimeHandler(pod.Spec.RuntimeClassName)\n// Passed to runtimeService.RunPodSandbox(..., runtimeHandler)\n```\n\n**Use Cases**:\n- GPU workloads (nvidia, amd handlers)\n- Kata Containers for isolation\n- Custom runtime configurations\n\n**Risk**: Handler misconfiguration silently falls back to default; wrong isolation if handler unavailable\n\n### 5.3 Device Plugin Integration\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/cm/device_manager`\n\nDevice managers expose custom resources:\n\n```go\ncontainerManager.UpdatePluginResources(nodeInfo, podAdmitAttributes)\n// Returns allocated devices via:\ncontainerManager.GetResources(ctx, pod, container)\n```\n\n**Extension Pattern**:\n1. Plugin registers via kubelet plugin watcher\n2. Device manager discovers available resources\n3. On pod admission: topology manager allocates devices\n4. On container creation: GetResources returns mounts, env vars, annotations\n\n**Risk**: Plugin not responding causes pod admission hangs; resource leaks if plugin crashes\n\n### 5.4 Pre-Admit Handlers\n\n**File**: Referenced by `containerManager.GetAllocateResourcesPodAdmitHandler()`\n\nPods are admitted before scheduling (kubelet-local gate):\n\n```\nPodAdmitHandler is called:\n  Before any containers created\n  With pod and all current active pods\n  Can reject pod or modify resource allocations\n```\n\n**Use Cases**:\n- Custom admission control (security policies)\n- Dynamic capacity tracking beyond kubelet-reported\n- Pod resource overcommit enforcement\n\n**Risk**: Rejecting pods here bypasses API server validation; inconsistent with scheduler decisions\n\n### 5.5 Image Credential Providers\n\n**File**: `pkg/kubelet/images/pullmanager`\n\nCustom executables can provide registry credentials:\n\n```\nCredential Provider Plugin:\n  Input: image reference\n  Output: username/password or bearer token\n  Executed as subprocess during image pull\n  Can be slow (synchronous in critical path)\n```\n\n**Risk**: Slow credential providers delay all pod startup; credential exposure in logs if not careful\n\n## 6. Operational Implications\n\n### 6.1 Performance Characteristics\n\n**Container Startup Latency**\n- Sandbox creation: 10-50ms (depends on network namespace setup, cgroup creation)\n- Image pull: seconds to minutes (network bound, registry latency)\n- Container creation: 50-200ms per container (runtime-dependent)\n- Container start: 0-5000ms (application dependent, health probe startup)\n- **Total first pod**: seconds to minutes (dominated by image pull)\n- **Total subsequent pod** (cached image): hundreds of milliseconds\n\n**Resource Manager Overhead**\n- CPU manager affinity computation: O(n) where n = CPU count\n- Topology manager hint collection: 3-5ms per container (gathers from CPU/memory/device managers)\n- Cgroup creation: ~1-10ms per pod (filesystem operations)\n- **Scaling limit**: ~100 containers on single node before noticeable delay\n\n**Garbage Collection Overhead**\n- Lists all containers from runtime: O(n) where n = total containers ever created\n- Typical: <100ms for nodes with ~100 total containers\n- **Risk**: GC pause blocks pod operations if many dead containers\n\n### 6.2 Reliability and Resilience\n\n**Recovery from Kubelet Restart**\n- On restart, kubelet queries runtime for all pods and containers\n- Containers continue running under runtime; kubelet re-syncs state\n- **Safe**: Long-running pods unaffected; state consistent from runtime ground truth\n- **Risk**: If container runtime crashed, kubelet cannot detect state; assumes runtime recovery\n\n**Handling Hung Container Runtime**\n- All CRI calls timeout after default 2 minutes\n- Timeout causes pod to be marked failed; events recorded\n- **Limitation**: Cannot forcefully kill runtime; depends on external monitoring\n\n**Orphaned Containers**\n- If kubelet crashes, containers keep running\n- Upon restart: kubelet reattaches via container ID matching\n- **Risk**: If containers deleted from runtime externally, kubelet has stale state (pod never terminates)\n\n### 6.3 Resource Accounting and Limits\n\n**Memory Pressure Handling**\n- Node eviction manager uses cgroup memory.max + usage to trigger eviction\n- Container manager enforces memory.max at pod scope\n- **Limitation**: No memory overcommit enforcement; OOMKiller is last resort\n- **Tuning**: `--memory-manager-policy=static` for guaranteed allocations\n\n**CPU Throttling**\n- CPU limits enforced via cpu.cfs_quota_us / cpu.cfs_period_us\n- CPU manager reserves CPUs for Guaranteed pods (no sharing)\n- **Limitation**: Noisy neighbor problem if bursting pods on same node\n- **Tuning**: `--cpu-manager-policy=static` + CPU topology hints\n\n**Disk I/O Limits**\n- Not enforced by container manager (delegated to runtime/systemd)\n- Cleanup policy for images/logs relies on simple age/count heuristics\n- **Risk**: Slow disk I/O can stall pod startup\n\n### 6.4 Observability and Debugging\n\n**Event Recording**\n- Pod events recorded on key transitions (SandboxChanged, ContainerBackOff, etc.)\n- Events help operators understand failure reasons\n- **Limitation**: No structured logs of internal decisions; must read kubelet logs\n\n**Metrics Exported**\n- `kubelet_pod_worker_duration_seconds`: Time to sync pod\n- `kubelet_container_manager_*`: Resource manager metrics (CPU affinity, etc.)\n- `kubelet_runtime_crio_operations_duration_seconds`: CRI call latencies (per operation type)\n- **Gap**: No metrics on sandbox/container state mismatches\n\n**Pod Log Access**\n- Kubelet reads container logs from runtime via GetContainerLogs\n- Supports `--tail` and `--follow` for streaming\n- **Limitation**: Logs lost if container removed before access\n\n### 6.5 Platform-Specific Behaviors\n\n**Linux-Specific**\n- Cgroup v1 (deprecated) vs. v2 (recommended) have different resource enforcement\n- User namespaces supported (UserNamespacesSupport feature gate)\n- Shared PID namespace across pod supported (ShareProcessNamespace)\n- **Deprecation Warning**: cgroup v1 usage logged; migration path to v2 required\n\n**Windows-Specific** (File: `pkg/kubelet/cm/container_manager_windows.go`)\n- No cgroup support; uses Windows Job Objects for resource limits\n- Memory limits via Job Object limits\n- CPU shares via Job scheduling (no precise pinning like Linux)\n- Network isolation via HNS (Host Networking Service)\n- **Limitation**: No fine-grained CPU affinity; device management different\n\n**macOS (Not Supported for Production)**\n- Minimal runtime support; used for local development only\n- No container isolation (containers run as local processes)\n- Not tested in CI; not recommended for real workloads\n\n## 7. File and Directory Map\n\n### Core Runtime Interfaces\n- `pkg/kubelet/container/runtime.go` (450 lines)\n  - `Runtime` interface: SyncPod, KillPod, GarbageCollect, image operations\n  - Container/Pod/Status type definitions\n  - GC policy, port mapping, mount, device types\n\n- `pkg/kubelet/container/container_gc.go` (50 lines)\n  - Wrapper around Runtime.GarbageCollect with policies\n\n### Runtime Implementation\n- `pkg/kubelet/kuberuntime/` (30+ files)\n  - `kuberuntime_manager.go` (1500+ lines): Main orchestration, SyncPod logic\n  - `kuberuntime_container.go` (500+ lines): Container creation, config generation\n  - `kuberuntime_sandbox.go` (400+ lines): Pod sandbox creation, network setup\n  - `kuberuntime_gc.go` (400+ lines): Garbage collector for containers/sandboxes\n  - `kuberuntime_image.go`: Image pull, list, remove operations\n  - `instrumented_services.go`: Metrics/tracing wrapper around CRI calls\n\n### Resource Management\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/container_manager.go` (320 lines)\n  - `ContainerManager` interface: Start, GetResources, UpdateQOSCgroups, etc.\n\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/container_manager_linux.go` (1000+ lines)\n  - `containerManagerImpl` struct: Cgroup management, system containers\n  - `NewContainerManager`: Factory with cadvisor, subsystem detection\n\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/pod_container_manager_linux.go` (300+ lines)\n  - `PodContainerManager` interface: Exists, EnsureExists, Destroy\n  - Pod-level cgroup setup, QoS hierarchy\n\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/qos_container_manager_linux.go` (300+ lines)\n  - Top-level QoS cgroup management (guaranteed, burstable, besteffort)\n\n### Resource Managers (Pluggable)\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/cpumanager/` (500+ lines)\n  - `Manager` interface: AddContainer, RemoveContainer, Status\n  - Policies: none, static\n  - State: assignment tracking per pod/container\n\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/memorymanager/` (400+ lines)\n  - Similar structure to CPU manager\n  - Memory affinity for NUMA\n\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/devicemanager/` (300+ lines)\n  - Device plugin discovery and allocation\n\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/topologymanager/` (400+ lines)\n  - Topology hints coordination between managers\n  - Hint aggregation and conflict resolution\n\n### Lifecycle Hooks\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/internal_container_lifecycle.go` (60 lines)\n  - `InternalContainerLifecycle` interface: PreCreateContainer, PreStartContainer, PostStopContainer\n  - Implementation coordinating CPU/memory/topology managers\n\n### Supporting Types\n- `pkg/kubelet/cm/types.go`: CgroupName, CgroupManager interface, Status types\n- `pkg/kubelet/container/cache.go`: Container hash caching for update detection\n- `pkg/kubelet/container/sync_result.go`: SyncResult aggregation for pod operations\n\n## 8. Summary: Critical Invariants and Design Principles\n\n### Invariants\n1. **One Sandbox Per Pod**: Each pod has exactly one sandbox (network namespace) that all containers share\n2. **Container Order Matters**: Init containers must complete before app containers start; ephemeral containers anytime\n3. **State Convergence**: SyncPod drives pod toward desired state through repeated calls; idempotent\n4. **Cgroup Hierarchy**: Pod cgroups nested under QoS cgroups nested under kubelet cgroup\n5. **GC Safety**: Dead containers only removed if age > minAge AND not from untracked pods (unless forced)\n\n### Design Principles\n1. **Stateless Manager**: Kubelet container manager queries runtime ground truth; no in-memory pod state\n2. **Graceful Termination**: Respect application shutdown; escalate to SIGKILL only after grace period\n3. **Resource Isolation**: QoS guarantees enforced via cgroups; no application-level throttling\n4. **Pluggable Resources**: CPU/memory/device managers plugged via hooks; topology coordinated\n5. **Progressive Pod Lifecycle**: Containers created sequentially per dependencies; not all-or-nothing\n6. **Observability**: Events and metrics on key transitions; ground truth from container runtime\n\nThese principles and invariants ensure Kubernetes pods behave predictably across diverse node configurations and container runtimes while maintaining isolation and resource fairness.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
